Original fifth-back returns and suppressed adjacent doublets remain constraints, not a decoder. The within-word lag-five surplus survives page-specific bigram conditioning and fixed-cipher word-length shuffles, but the alignment of actual word cuts is compatible with editorial/source formatting as well as a word-local codec; it does not identify an operation ([conditional/page-Markov gate](../im-results/lp2-original-lagfive-conditional-page-markov-gate.md); [fixed-cipher wordbreak gate](../im-results/lp2-fixedcipher-wordbreak-lagfive-source-gate.md)). The strongest source-motivated reversible candidates are now bounded: the p7 five-dot word-reset period-five additive and five-rail models fail the joint lag-five/adjacent-repeat native controls, while exhaustive all-secret five-tap linear feedback fails on original LP2 and held-out pages ([source-constrained encoder gate](../im-results/lp2-lagfive-source-constrained-encoder-gate.md); [all-secret five-tap exclusion](../im-results/lp2-fivetap-fullsecret-spectral-native-gate.md)). The p50 32×8/base60 successor graph now has a source-motivated, independently reproduced navigation route: the authenticated 2016 window `EPIPHANY / SEEKS / THE / DEVOTED`, with GP0 values `[121,71,20,102]`, reaches p32 in 23 forward row-major steps from state 71; p32's original scan independently contains 121 rune glyphs, and `EMPTY` maps to 92 at step 7; this was identified after intersecting the selected path with signed words, so it is not a pre-registered holdout. A fresh original-scan transcription and independent 62,720-configuration search reproduce the unique base60/zero-based route. This is navigation, not unknown-page plaintext or key recovery. The 2+11 graph is deterministic. The first-29-of-43 register is superseded: #2045 identifies a plausible 29-word body bounded by the salutation and coda. Its q/r use and index origin remain hypotheses, and the corrected direct q/r, GP0 and H consumers fail the p0 key gate ([p50 graph report](../im-results/lp2-p50-functional-graph-2016-wordmap.md); [independent reproduction](../sims/lp2_p50_agent1_independent_repro.py); [source-selector audit #2018](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/2018)). The authenticated 2013 BASE29/DH text specifies numeral conversion and a participant-selected DH exchange, not a per-rune stream cipher; the p56 prime/F-transparent cipher remains page-specific and its tested global/page/chapter transfers fail ([2013 signed source](../resources/external/2013-tcp-server-signed-source.md); [p56 transfer gate](../im-results/lp2-p56-globalconjugation-two-longword-allsecret-original-gate.md)). No complete source-selected reversible state model is currently available. Follow-up Issue #1989 audited the additional LP1 p5 square/moth and four-apostrophe cues: the p5 lag-five difference and LP1 apostrophe-transparent Vigenère transfers are already negative, while the apostrophe glyph supports at most punctuation preservation, not a lag-five state update, key, or receiver. Do not expand generic five-phase or feedback searches without a source-selected operation and receiver. See the [Issue #1981 audit](../im-results/lp2-issue1981-crosssource-state-model-audit.md) and [Issue #1989 selector audit](../im-results/lp2-issue1989-new-source-selector-audit.md). The original **256 colored/punctuation events** are physically supported but do not uniquely select a tape/order/operation; a direct table XOR and all permutations as an ordinary GP prose receiver fail their defined tests ([original-event study](../im-results/lp2-red256-table-permutation-invariant.md)). The alleged native p51 OutGuess 30,102-byte extraction is a matched-clean-cover pseudopayload, not a certified hidden message ([#1428](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1428)); native historical JSteg-Shell framing is absent on the original 58 LP2 scans under its documented headers ([#1402](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1402)). A complete original-byte compressed-domain gate now shows that deleting the single identical 2,594-byte ICC APP2 segment from each scan makes **all 58 files byte-for-byte identical to coefficient-preserving optimized-libjpeg reserialization**. Independent standard-marker audit #1644 reaches the same container-level conclusion from the source bytes: all 58 LP2 JPEGs are baseline 3600×2400 one-scan files with the same JFIF/Artifex-sRGB APP family, one color/grayscale DQT family, zero COM or other metadata payload, and zero post-EOI bytes. p27/JPEG44, p50/JPEG67 and p55/JPEG72 are ordinary members of those templates; all 58 DHT signatures are individually distinct, so target-page Huffman uniqueness is not exceptional ([marker audit](../im-results/lp2-jpeg-standard-marker-audit.md); [SFT](../sims/lp2_jpeg_standard_marker_inventory_sft.py); [exact per-file offset inventory](../sims/lp2_jpeg_container_offset_inventory_sft.py); [#1644](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1644)). Thus optimized Huffman tables, entropy serialization, scan padding and post-EOI bytes contain no independent per-page payload. A paired-frequency screen also rejects dense sign-magnitude AC-LSB replacement across every nonoverlapping 20,000-eligible-coefficient source window (minimum chi-square/df **2.569022**), while deterministic planted replacements on p50/p55/p57 fall to **0.890680/1.044514/0.927183**. Sparse/F5/matrix, other coefficient-selection rules and spatial steganography remain unexcluded ([exact source SFT and scope](../im-results/lp2-jpeg-canonical-libjpeg-and-lsb-stego-gate.md)).

**Original thirteen-dot mark-local stationary predecessor gate (#1470).** The 28 authentic p0–55 thirteen-dot marks define 26 nonempty candidate reset intervals. Allowing each span arbitrary secret independent predecessor-conditioned GP29 row bijections and one free first rune, a tight rearrangement-invariant plaintext IoC ceiling rejects all tested native-GP source windows on **five long intervals (9,494 original runes, 73.28%)**; for the 3,008-rune interval the exact maximum is .043670929, below the reference minimum .055969360. A separate all-key top-k majorization test excludes a sixth 1,005-rune interval when matching the source control rune and word counts (**six intervals, 10,499 runes, 81.04%**). All 2,896 printed word slots remain independently lexicon-feasible, with the 32 cross-page words preserved, so the joint sentence constraint remains open. The stationary mark-local one-back bijection family has no ordinary-GP control passage decoder under these tests. This leaves nonstationary mechanisms, other output representations and untested English passages unresolved; no unknown LP2 plaintext or author-selected key follows ([exact two-test decision and Windows controls](../im-results/lp2-13dot-mark-local-predecessor-allsecret-gate.md)). **Thirteen-dot reset-boundary gate (#1505):** the broader reset interpretation now also fails a direct ciphertext-structure test. Across 24 eligible unique 13-dot boundaries, 64-rune before/after changes are ordinary for adjacent equality (**matched p=0.1583**) and lag-five returns (**p=0.7628**); transition-difference JS is only a marginal single-metric effect (**p=0.0388**) and exceeds same-chapter ordinary-boundary means in **5/8** chapters. More decisively, restarting the validated p50 low-value clock at each 13-dot boundary collapses its repeat association from **33/3025 vs 53/9876, OR 2.0555, p=0.001349** to **23/2975 vs 63/9926, OR 1.2363, p=0.2428**. Page and visual-chapter resets erase it further. Treat the stencil as structural punctuation unless a separate source clue specifies another use; do not use it as a generic cipher-state restart ([report](../im-results/lp2-thirteendot-reset-boundary-gate.md); [SFT](../sims/lp2_thirteendot_reset_boundary_sft.cjs); [PASS 44396116739](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/44396116739); [Director #1505](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1505)).

**Recently resolved source-operator candidates.** The p3–7 original red-span initials really read `LPAD`, but no source gives a pad string, unique width or complete inverse; the source p23 five-dot/Aquila fit is a postselected geometric approximation rather than an authenticated connected-star walk ([LPAD report](../workplace/lp2-p3to7-red-initial-lpad-readout.md), [#1435](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1435), [#1452](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1452)). Historical extraction, source-crib, book-walk, artwork, punctuation and standalone-table screens #1434–#1450 did not select a reversible unknown-page cipher ([Director issue search](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues?q=is%3Aissue+label%3Adirector-task+is%3Aclosed)). The eight bounded tasks **#1451–#1458** all closed by 10:11 UTC on 18 November, with no unknown plaintext. Their surviving decisions are narrow: frozen p50 KDF, H·G quotient and prime-D byte-object consumers fail; the memoryless word-break side-channel lacks the information needed to recover all 86 exceptions; source-attributed insect/trilobite art and LPAD still lack uniquely specified original operands and operations; a [direct source-image comparison](../im-results/lp2-p2-schroeter-source-image-comparison.md) now weakens the specific p2 Schröter-figure-copy premise; the T6 statistic persists without a plaintext decoder ([Critic consolidated closure assessment](../communications/broadcast/20261118T1031Z_critic_eight-closures-source-selection-priority.md)). Original p50 quotient/nine-alphabet/261-state row-register and other complete bounded controls are documented in the [exact source-side report](../im-results/lp2-p50-quotient9-sidechannel-original-gate.md). A contemporaneous 7 January 2013 participant Pastebin record, AfndPJa0, labels reversible primes with `*` and ordinary primes with `+`; it records `The Instar Emergence →761*`, `Gematria Primus →641+`, and the three p57 parable clause sums. A distinct 7 January 2013 IRC transcript, linked by the contemporaneous [Instar-emergence article](https://uncovering-cicada.fandom.com/wiki/Instar_emergence_%28mp3_and_hidden_poem%29) to Pastebin XQ9rJSad as its full log, independently records first-onion COUNT reports `a=97*`, `b=61+`, `ab=158`, `ae=101*`, `o=7*`, and `eo=41+`. The `ab=158` item is a test of the literal phrase `ab`: the command help lists `count [phrase]`, and participant `soulseekah` immediately says “so it did add them up” before repeating `97+61`; 158 is the additive GP value of the two letters, not the 158-printed-word inventory of LP2 p0–p2 and not a selector for that address space. These marker observations agree with `*` for primes whose decimal reversals are prime, `+` for prime-only values, and no suffix for composites. The mirror is pinned by Git blob SHA-1 `2d865faeec179d6b5984643d8b2af7efa3269f73` (399,787 bytes; archive commit 8 May 2021). One `j` exchange is ambiguous (Atlas_ posts `37`/`+`, while crash_demons reports `37*`) and is excluded. This is independent participant-reported evidence, not raw TCP or authenticated daemon code; it weakens but does not eliminate the possibility of participant-side annotation. The later 2022 reconstruction reverses the marker legend. The often-cited `count parable →449*` appears only in the later reconstruction, not in the nine-item original paste. Thus the clear p57 sums predict `PARABLE →449+` and `FIND →151*` under the participant convention, but neither p57 input is recorded in the primary 2013 paste. The [primary-record-corrected carried direction inverse](../im-results/lp2-count2013-primary-marker-direction-gate.md) tested its one fixed original rule against 174,700,160 full-library screen decodes on two source windows (both pointer orientations), giving best first300 GP29 IoC 1.3404, below the predeclared 1.40; no unknown plaintext or creator-authored decoder. The source still does not map labels to cipher movement. **Global word-length exception-channel gate (#1463, 18 November).** The signed 2016 message does not specify a code over word lengths, a global permutation/order, or a state clock. The signed 2013 NEXT payload's 406-word GP-length sequence was also tested: its exact maximum is eight words and no qualified fuzzy continuation survives; the protocol treats NEXT as client-supplied text storage, not an LP2 key selector. The signed body is a membership letter that explicitly denies hidden codes or secret messages; its 406 words occupy 11 prose paragraphs, not a 14×29 layout.  The 2,896-word length multiset has 8,791.876 bits of theoretical ordering capacity, but that is not a decoder. The #1456 forward adjacent-length-difference channel carries only 56.154 bits and leaves 505.543 conditional bits; the centered three-word variant has 276.120 empirical bits but fails its within-chapter shuffle gate (p=0.14495). The natural external LP1-codebook/word-length/GP-prime direction reading was tested in 4,176 page-head decodes with planted controls but no qualifying original output. Close #1463 at missing source selector, not as a universal impossibility theorem ([report](../im-results/lp2-wordlength-global-code-source-selector-gate.md); [external R37 gate](https://github.com/Dukotah/cicada3301/blob/6b945291ed298c14ccf8c73603f695a28f6a7e35/liber-primus/MEASURED-CEILING.md)). A stronger key-independent **complete-register BWT gate** now applies to the source-matched February 2015 729-rune prime-difference intermediate and its four frozen standard GP29 move-to-front outputs: their stable LF mappings have unequal cycle lengths, so **none can be the complete last column of a standard 729-symbol sentinel-free circular BWT, for any primary index**; genuine 729-rune BWT/MTF plants pass ([exact cycle proof, inputs and controls](../im-results/lp2-2014-html133331-bwt-2015D-lfcycles-original-gate.md), [source MTF family](../im-results/lp2-2015-primeD-gp29-mtf-original-gate.md), [#1477](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1477)). This excludes only the stipulated whole-register transform and leaves separately framed or value-changing operations open. The #1472 full-corpus word-length multiset-rank readout reproduces [the pre-existing 13 November calculation](../im-results/lp2-wordlength-multiset-rank-binary-gate.md), so its new implementation adds independent reproducibility rather than a new cipher exclusion ([#1472 correction](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1472)). The remaining priority is a source-selected complete operation and authentic withheld plaintext, not further parameter rotations of these failed consumers.

**GP0 successor to the 2016 word-map gate.** The later GP0→p50 navigation mechanism summarized near the top of this README supersedes only the older claim that the signed 2016 sentence lacked an executable word-number traversal. The bounded GP-prime/p32/literal-address negatives below remain valid for their tested grammars.

**2016 signed word/map/road/direction source gate (#1462/#1654, 18 November).** The only first-party word→number operation found is the 2014 LP1 p05 conversion of each printed GP rune-word to the sum of its published prime weights; it reproduces all 25 cells of the third 5×5 numerical square. The original Onion6 form uses that square as a submission operand rather than a traversal. Neither that stage nor the signed 2016 sentence supplies a compass conversion, start, movement unit, word selector or stop. Direct word-sum addresses into the 11×11 p32 map fail the original p33/p55 range controls, and the solved-p57 coordinate/p32-readout controls are non-prose. **#1654 adds a literal lexical control:** among its requested semantic terms, exact GP `WORDS` and `NUMBERS` both point uniquely to the solved Warning phrase `EITHER THE WORDS OR THEIR NUMBERS`; broader exact echoes include `THE WAY` and `AND YOU WILL` in Welcome and `SEEC` in p56. All ten requested terms have zero exact complete-word hits in the 2,896 unknown p0-p55 words. Their ordinary one-based signed-word positions select physical pages 3,5,6,9,16,19,21,24,27,30, which never reach p50 or solved p56/p57 and carry no source-authored walk/extraction grammar. The lexical echoes therefore support the signed message as a broad instruction about Liber Primus's recovered words and numbers, plausibly relevant after plaintext recovery, while the ordinary literal-address branch is closed ([literal semantic route gate](../im-results/lp2-signed2016-literal-semantic-route-gate.md) · [SFT](../sims/lp2_signed2016_literal_semantic_route_sft.cjs) · [2016 manuscript grammar gate](../im-results/lp2-signed2016-manuscript-grammar-source-gate.md) · [physical word-navigation gate](../im-results/lp2-2016-physical-word-navigation-exact-original-gate.md)).

**Terminal-pair / p50 token-bracket convergence (Agent16, 18 November).** The finite 2013 `prime_echo` route independently selects the intact terminal `DJU / BEI` pair and its unique duplicate on p27. Under the existing provisional GP-prime / 58-page word-address rule, `DJU` reaches **p50 local2** through p13 `NAEWG`, while adjacent `BEI` merges into the same `DJU` node and reaches the same cell. The authenticated 2016 word `lies` already selects adjacent **p50 local3** under that rule. The two cells are the printed tokens **`3L,3D`**; their only other table copies occur in reverse, yielding the radix-independent unique nested token bracket **`3L 3D [43 tokens] 3D 3L`** at globals82..128. Its midpoint is global105, the table's sole printed **`13`** token. Under conventional base60 only, the first enclosed token `2r=173` selects clear p57 **THE**, then **FOLD -> SB -> END**; the drop-f base59 fork selects an opaque p54 word instead. A direct historical `FIRFUMFERENFE` additive consumer of the 43 values is nonlinguistic. This is a stronger conditional navigation junction and a codec-sensitive semantic continuation. It supplies no unknown plaintext and does not authenticate the 58-page address grammar. [Consolidated report](../im-results/lp2-terminal-p50-token-bracket-handoff.md) · [SFT](../sims/lp2_terminal_p50_bracket_wordmap_sft.cjs) · [Windows PASS 44403562261](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/44403562261).

**2014 Stage5 source recovery (Agent12, 18 November; historical control, not an LP2 key).** The archived `avowyfgl5lkzfj3n.onion/post/index.html` gzip object has now been reconstructed byte-exactly as four original 2400×3600 JPEGs, SHA-matched to LP1 archive images 06–09. Pinned OutGuess 0.4 on recovered image-03 yields the exact 140-byte historical message; its 56-character body decodes under the documented seven-column permutation to the quote `TOBELIEVETRUTHISTODESTROYPOSSIBILITY`, the next v2 onion `q4utgdi2n4m4uim5.onion`, and four trailing digits `9133` (consistent with four transposition padding cells, not an authenticated key). The exact quote was then frozen as the *single* OutGuess password on adjacent image-04 and all 58 original LP2 JPEGs; all outputs were high-entropy/no-signature and the same apparent header occurred on a payload-free control. This recovers a genuine 2014 player path and rejects this one source-phrase→OutGuess LP2 transfer; it supplies no unknown LP2 plaintext or key ([full bytes, exact extraction, controls and CI evidence](../im-results/2014-stage5-avowy-archive-audit.md)).

The prior direct GP-index-to-prime-offset giant-red-initial key (#1410), original 256-colored-event selection (#1411/#1282), six-rune cuneiform BASE29 key (#1413), p27 fifteen-face wing, red13/2014-title route, p0–2 23-cycle, 3008=32×94 table alignment, 36-dot stencil, PARABLE/DH73 direct receiver, word-edge transducer and cuneiform typed-BASE29 code (#1399, #1414, #1416–#1419, #1421, #1423) remain only **scoped negative gates**. The completed 2016 physical-layout historical-key campaign [#1348](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1348) tested 22 source-inequivalent layout-sign rules through the finite 581-key anti-repeat skip/coin screen; all 18 new jobs completed, all 270 whole-window candidates failed and the highest first-300 score was 1.397, below its prespecified 1.40 bar ([exact gate](../im-results/lp2-2016-layout18-unfinished-source-gate.md)). A typed unknown-page decoder or source-selected key remains missing. Full forward reencryption is an implementation invariant, not an original-cipher solution.

This is the **authoritative, decision-focused research entry point**. It consolidates the strongest findings and their limits, rather than reprinting each past experiment. The prior full-length synthesis is preserved as a [dated research archive](../im-results/archive-results-readme-20261113-before-critic-consolidation.md); its older claims must be read through the corrected conclusions here. Exact tests and raw evidence remain in linked reports, source files and [the active inversion challenge](../workplace/integrated-original-inversion-challenge.md).

**Corrected original p0 red-ink boundary (16 November, JPEG 17.jpg):** The opening contains **eight red runes (including a giant initial), an ordinary small red word separator, five additional red runes, and the large red 13-dot marker at rune offset 13**. Black body prose begins afterward with THJGAE-FJOE. The two red title words SHEOGMIAF-SYENGC (8+5 runes), original transcription, and older derivative p0 ink/large-mark metadata all agree. A same-day interpretation had mistakenly counted the 12 smaller red runes as seven, assigning five red runes to the black body and moving the marker to offset 8. That interpretation is **retracted**. The [corrected original-image adjudication](../im-results/lp2-p0-red13-13dot-blackbody-original-source-adjudication.md), [pixel SFT](../sims/lp2_p0_red13_marker13_original_image_sft.py), [superseded original note](../im-results/lp2-p0-red8-13dot-black5-original-source-correction.md), and [corrected physical-ink overlay](../resources/liber-primus/lp2-p0-physical-ink-override.json) identify the boundary reproducibly. No subtraction of five runes from the previously reported 225-red-rune derivative corpus is justified by p0; the annotation-derived 31 thirteen-dot entries comprise 28 chapter 13-dot marks plus three additional solved-page marks on pp56–57; the companion punctuation inventory has 31 chapter marks (28×13, 3×10). Thus 225+31=256 is a physically confirmed original-image event count, while its assembly as a combined 256-symbol cryptographic register remains unauthenticated. A frozen direct XOR of that candidate tape (GP prime value per red rune, 13 per 13-dot mark) against the p50 256-byte table yields no readable page, common file signature, or p56 128-hex hash target's preimage; this rejects only that direct one-time-pad consumer, not all possible downstream uses ([reproducer and exact output](../im-results/lp2-grid256-allsecret-keyclock-original-gate.md#separate-256-event-red13-dot-tape-as-a-direct-p50-xor-pad)). A separate **all-secret event-permutation invariant** retires every pure rearrangement of these 256 original red-rune/13-dot events (including any p50-byte-sorted or secretly routed order), even with one global GP29 alphabet relabeling, as a *direct ordinary GP prose* receiver: all transformations preserve the original **225-rune IoC 0.03321429**, whereas all **2,755 pinned solved-source 225-rune windows** have IoC ≥**0.05321429**. The numeric table may still supply a value-changing key or encode a different register, but permuting colored events alone cannot reproduce the observed solved-author language frequency profile ([source-native proof and passing Windows Node22 run](../im-results/lp2-red256-table-permutation-invariant.md)). The conditional SECUENDI / five-sign R-A-T-I-O arithmetic has no authenticated key or solved continuation; the five-sign suffix also conflicts with the source-native dedicated IO-rune spelling, and the false marker-at8 mode-switch premise remains retired. **P0 red-title DH candidate now has an independent p32 exponent prediction (16 November; Agent4 handoff 19 November):** the two original red words have GP-prime sums **347** and **315**, giving residues **2** and **16** modulo the signed 2013 worked group (p=23,g=5); their discrete logs are **2 and8**, with shared scalar **3**. The later YOU33 repair now supplies those ordered exponents before p0 is opened: p32's exact 11×11 boundary33 is `[K,A]=[2,8]`; canonical zero-based row-major coordinate `(2,8)` returns `F=0`, and the same boundary starts the manuscript-unique literal `HOME` row, selecting p0. Among the 19 canonical adjacent 8|5 word pairs, only the actual p0 red title has the predicted ordered residues `(2,16)` / logs `(2,8)`. This materially upgrades the red-title DH operand interpretation as a cross-page state handoff. The signed 2013 protocol still does not specify a GP29 KDF/serialization from shared scalar3, so the symmetric consumer remains unresolved. [p32→p0 handoff](../im-results/lp2-p32-home-p0-dh23-handoff.md) · [handoff SFT](../sims/lp2_p32_home_p0_dh23_handoff_sft.cjs). The simplest group-native expansion, K_i=3·5^i mod23 (period 22), was tested as a continuous additive key over p1–55: that 12,694-rune payload is exactly 22×577, yet both orbit directions and both signs remain nonprose (best native-GP trigram −11.86586 versus a same-length Gibran-window minimum −8.09449). This rejects that direct group-orbit consumer, not the red-only DH arithmetic; no source-selected KDF/GP29 serialization is established. Using 3 to start the authentic prime-totient stream at prime 3 gives first key φ(3)=2 and maps first black p0 rune TH (index 2) to plaintext F (index 0), which violates the verified plaintext-F-transparent forward rule if that cipher is applied to p0. The direct p56-family consumer is therefore rejected; no p0 plaintext or key is recovered. This K3 red-only reading competes with a separate K18 full-heading/body-assisted interpretation of the same heading; they are not independent confirmations or cipher layers ([source gate](../im-results/p0-dh23-red-heading-exchange.md), [original-input SFT](../sims/p0_red_wordpair_dh23_key_sft.cjs), [verified F-skip control](../im-results/lp2-ftransparent-word-lexical-viterbi.md)). The frozen red13-as-13-bonacci initializer over the 716-rune black body round-trips but remains nonprose (IoC 0.03485 versus source 0.03451); no source selects that IV/update law, so this exact candidate is retired ([closed #1220](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1220)). The global red-only stream gate #1240 found no source-selected color-transition cipher; the original red elements have different rubric/paragraph roles, and p56's red heading is ciphertext while p57's is clear. This corroborates the completed color-mode gate #1055, not a universal claim that red has no puzzle significance.

**Original-image large punctuation census (Agent 1, 16 November 2026):** Direct component analysis of **all 58 original 2400×3600 JPEGs** reproduces the physical counts **30 red thirteen-dot marks, one black thirteen-dot mark (p7), four red ten-dot marks and one red 23-dot mark**, exactly **36** large marks. This establishes the source's 31 *physical thirteen-dot* selection and distinguishes it from the 31 *chapter marks* (28 thirteen-dot and three ten-dot) in the transcription inventory. A second original-image connected-component SFT recovers **225 red rune glyphs** across 15 pages after excluding nine red decimal digits and resolving one joined GP29 glyph pair. Thus **225 red runes plus 31 thirteen-dot marks = 256 physically verified colored/punctuation events**. A cryptographic event ordering and consumer are still unauthenticated.

**The original 256-event selection is nonunique (#1278/#1282, 16 November):** Three distinct, source-visible classes each give 256 elements: A = 225 red runes + all 31 physical thirteen-dot marks (including the black p7 mark); B = 225 red runes + the 30 red thirteen-dot marks + the red p57 terminal 23-dot mark (excluding black p7); C = 225 red runes + all 31 chapter marks (28 thirteen-dot and three ten-dot, including a black mark). Their event objects, ordering and final-table-cell alignments differ, so neither the 256 count nor the clear p57 terminal identifies a unique original receiver. A first-party class selector and value-changing table consumer remain absent. **Combined-register receiver gate #1652 (Agent8, 18 November)** re-audits this branch after the p27/DH23 state was recovered and finds that the extra scalars still supply no typed operation: signed DH ends at an integer with no KDF/vector receiver; p57 circumference shedding is geometric rather than a 256-value inverse; direct event/table XOR, GP29 mask transfer, fixed byte→event substitution, row/column affine pads, pure permutations and five exact AES-key constructions are already negative. Applying DH fields 2/6/15 as repeated keys, modulo23 reducers, seeds or clocks would invent the missing schedule. **Retire the combined 256-event × p50 × DH hypothesis until a first-party clue fixes event encoding, state update, inverse and target** ([gate](../im-results/lp2-red13-grid256-dh-value-operation-gate.md)) ([completed historical-selector gate #1282](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1282)). The frozen A-class 32-run base60 password `D0G030B00EC09B0G0J0A08030x905070` was tested once on all 58 original JPEGs with OpenPuff v4.00, A-only and JPEG Maximum 1/2; native extraction returned `corrupted stream` and produced no file. This rejects only that exact source-to-password/carrier/level choice, not other independently selected payload routes ([report](../workplace/lp2-red13dot-base60-openpuff32-source-route.md); [original native run](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/43879058108)). For A, the 32 thirteen-dot-delimited red-rune groups include 15 **empty** groups and only one group of seven red runes; therefore the simple 7+1 event-per-row interpretation fails actual source layout. None of the three event classes or any 32×8 consumer is author-selected, so the exact 256 count alone does not reveal a unique key, address clock or plaintext. [Original-event decision](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1278) · [image census](../im-results/lp2-original-dot-punctuation-physical-census.md). Extra red characters on p37 are decimal section numerals 2, 3 and 4, not red GP29 runes. The three physical stencil classes also have uniform original-pixel column signatures: 31 thirteen-dot marks are 4/5/4, four ten-dot marks are 3/4/3, and p57's unique 23-dot terminal is 4/5/5/5/4. The only marked pages totaling 36 dots are solved p56 (13+10+13) and clear p57 (13+23); p57's terminal 23 therefore equals p56's internal 10 plus terminal 13. This is a source-exact terminal-pair checksum candidate, not an authenticated cipher opcode or receiver. P57's 95-rune clear stream is also ten runes longer than p56's 85-rune cipher; a fixed five-rune-per-end peel, motivated by the circumference wording and leaving an 85-rune candidate, fails against p56's known plaintext, ciphertext and derived key stream, and as either additive GP29 pad (see the new page-edge SFT): the 23 mark follows p57's final punctuation, and the solved p56 prime-totient clock passes through its internal 13/10 marks without state change. ## 1. Authenticated plaintext and known methods

**Historically authenticated 2012 musical operation, with LP2 transfer excluded (17 November):** Both original Cicada MIDI text tracks encode exactly **1,272 source symbols** as each note’s pitch plus its sounding duration (24/96/192/384 ticks at 96 ticks/beat, 120 BPM). A full historical audio decoder recovers **all 558 + 714 original pairs and both complete known plaintexts** from exact-tone synthetic PCM and MP3-128k renderings. Source-pinned 2013 761.MP3 and 2014 *Interconnectedness* decoded under the literal same four-duration receiver (mono or stereo difference) recover **no unknown LP2 plaintext or key** under signed GP29 additive or calibrated R4 skip-aware original-cipher receivers with planted and shuffled controls. Retire only those original musical-key transfers; an independently sourced musical opcode remains possible. [Consolidated direct-source report](../im-results/lp2-audio-note-2012-positive-and-original-gate.md) · [historical waveform-positive CI](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/44020454768) · [original LP2 timing and stereo CI](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/44019647646).

- **Solved controls:** LP2 p56 is genuinely solved under the historical prime-totient cipher; p57 is 95 clear GP runes. The authentic cipher has a **transparent plaintext-F skip**: plaintext F emits F without consuming a key symbol, while a ciphertext F may also be produced by an encrypted non-F rune. An unchanged known-key lexical Viterbi now recovers **919/919 original solved-cipher runes**, including all **14 literal-F bypasses**, on LP1 WELCOME (515), LP2 p56 (85) and the **entire 319-rune LP1 second koan across pp14–15**, without supplying skip positions or known prose to the decoder. The koan's independently sourced `FIRFUMFERENFE` key passes its whole 88-word ciphertext with a continuous clock, and a separate solved-text assertion confirms 319/319. This strengthens the authentic decoder positive, **without finding an unknown LP2 key**. [Method and reproducible test](../im-results/lp2-ftransparent-word-lexical-viterbi.md) · [Passing manual Node22 CI](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/43734906770).

- **Global unknown-alphabet conjugation of the p56 prime/F law has exact original two-word all-secret obstructions under three source-visible clocks (18 November):** For any **single shared GP29 permutation π**, both signs and either literal-F or π(F) transparency, the 175-source-native-GP-word length-14 English reference gives **0 globally compatible candidate pairs** between intact original long words. Agent2's physical-page-reset theorem grants every shared initial prime index **s=0–463** and all physically possible earlier marker skips. Agent16's extensions keep the historically authentic **s=0** and independently test both the nine illustrated chapter starts **p0, p3, p8, p15, p23, p27, p33, p40, p54** and one continuous prime clock across all 12,956 unknown runes; all **60** sign/marker modes in each extension again yield zero shared-π pairs despite many local traces, while same-key English plants pass. This excludes these exact source-visible families without a 29! key search. Word-local clocks, independently sourced nonzero chapter/continuous starts, alternate plaintext registers and changed segmentation remain outside scope. [Consolidated original-input theorem](../im-results/lp2-p56-globalconjugation-two-longword-allsecret-original-gate.md) · [page-reset Windows PASS 44348958129](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/44348958129) · [chapter-reset Windows PASS 44372463051](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/44372463051) · [continuous-book Windows PASS 44374638976](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/44374638976) · [Issue #1492](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1492).
- **Authentic p56 prime/F decoder fails on eight of nine unknown chapters across 5,000 starting primes (15 November):** An exact dynamic program allows every plaintext-F nonconsuming skip and every starting prime-key index **0–4,999**. All first eight source-visible chapters, p0–2 through p40–53, have **zero** legally decodable complete GP29 strings under that authentic p56 model, regardless of natural-language assumptions. The final p54–55 chapter retains six candidate starts and eight exact 308-rune inverses, but all are near-uniform (GP29 IoC **0.03384–0.03589**) and none recovers actual prose. All nine already failed for key start zero at original offsets **235, 22, 30, 10, 0, 95, 58, 38, 105**. Full known p56 positive recovers/re-encrypts **85/85** including original true F skip; a nonzero-start positive passes too. The result excludes this concrete 5,000-start transferred mode on eight chapters, while independently supplied keys, internal resets or changed cipher modes remain open. The only 2014 reference to `59.txt`/`59.bin` is a 67-byte unsigned participant README, added 11 May and deleted 25 May; all 15 reachable Stage11 commits and the two documented archive families contain no 59 text/binary payload, and the signed Stage11 message/index do not mention it. The note's author and committer are Michel Oosterhof; the 11 May commit added only 56.bin, 56.txt and the README, and the 25 May deletion contains no 59 replacement or rename. This is not evidence that Cicada published a page 59 or that it was the p56 preimage; GitHub's public API cannot inspect dangling objects. - **Independent R43/F6 p56-hash sweep (3 November 2026):** the solved digest was tested as 64 bytes and 128 hex nibbles modulo 29 over all 55 unsolved pages, with both signs, Atbash and offsets 0–63 (28,160 decodes): **0/55 pages crossed** their standing bars; the best pmax was 4.596 against a page bar of 11.27. This closes that specified hash-keystream grid independently of the local direct-key pilot. - **Recovered contemporary 2014 solver code, without an unknown-page key:** The exact 18,283-byte original `cluosh/cicada_toolset/runeaylze/rcrypto.py` (Git blob `9a82a844d8574d2040eed60de76f4becdd77a666`) survives in 25 `cluosh`-authored May 4–June 3, 2014 commits. It provides GP29 parsing, four Vigenère variants, statistical tools and `key_from_primes`, but no original p0–55 deciphering key or plaintext; exact historical-code replay on original p0/p54 remains nonprose, whereas p56 recovers 85/85 under its known prime/F rules. **Two further source-dated participant programs have been recovered from still-live historical MediaFire IDs:** `UnRuneIt.jar` (15 May 2014, 766,148 bytes, SHA-256 `e465e31a5b46dbeeecae337caf1d195b17f6ab7c9596479b56b1f08690a4df16`) and `UnRuneIt2.jar` (19 May, 767,453 bytes, SHA-256 `b4a66f04837c368f04dab2a8573ab1da5d67b978f4cc4709c551f8913c900fb6`). Static original-bytecode inspection finds an interactive GP29 inverse `P=C−((prime mod29)−1)` whose prime list is supplied by the user, consuming one integer per ciphertext rune **without the authentic p56 plaintext-F bypass**. A bytecode-level input-contract audit of the original May 19 JAR shows its prompts explicitly require the operator to paste exactly N primes for the selected word; it has no baked-in prime list, automatic key generator or unknown-page recipient. [Exact UI/input contract](../im-results/lp2-2014-irc-nonpaste-file-source-gate.md#may-19-original-console-input-contract-source-locked-runtime-independent-bytecode-agent20-15-november). Neither JAR contains an unknown-page key, plaintext or missing key derivation; these are participant-authored tools rather than a Cicada instruction. The two original binary artifacts currently survive in a **seven-day-retention** CI download and via reproducible live-host retrieval, so their exact original bytes need a durable preservation decision. [Recovered 2014 Java programs, hashes and bytecode](../im-results/lp2-2014-irc-nonpaste-file-source-gate.md) · [original-byte CI artifact](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/43611434003). **Further original 30 July 2014 solver code recovered:** a complete [26,295-byte modified `rcrypto.py`](https://pastebin.com/raw/dGJSgN0m) and [2,576-byte `OEIS.py` helper](https://pastebin.com/raw/9U7SbHQE) survive at the exact original IRC L229856/860 URLs. The new p0 and p54 routines enumerate up to 245,722 OEIS candidate running keys, **without a source-selected winning ID or authenticated unknown-page plaintext**. The same author's [209-byte original marked p56 input](https://pastebin.com/raw/8cStVBG9) does reproducibly decrypt and re-encrypt **85/85** authentic p56 runes using the already-known prime-minus-one key and transparent F56. This confirms the original participant's handling of that solved control only; the unknown-page branches remain unconstrained guesses. [Recovered original-source code, exact digests, original dialogue and executable p56 positive](../im-results/lp2-2014-shaggydog-modified-rcrypto-oeis-gate.md) · [passing CI 43666638115](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/43666638115). No paste body or table was recovered. [PBPT source gate](../im-results/lp2-2014-irc-shared-code-original-acquisition.md#the-missing-nem0-pbpt-paste-archived-source-gate-agent20-15-november). This is not an original 2014 pad snapshot, and its non-rune prose/revision history remains unavailable. [Exact derivative](../im-results/2015-hj8pvq5s43-pad-derived-transcription.md). - **2013 whitespace ASCII is an authenticated-era precedent, not an LP2 decoder:** An original 2013 signed-shell text carrier contains 552 physical space/tab choices in four lines; mapping tab=1 and space=0 to eight-bit ASCII recovers the source-era `emiwp4muu2ktwknf.onion` and instructions. Those literal trailing whitespace bits are **not authenticated by OpenPGP canonicalized signatures**. No original 2014–16 instruction maps LP2's printed 3-/4-/10-/13-dot punctuation to the same carrier or fixes an octet clock. Original LP2 text geometry shows mechanically fixed 188/189px line pitch and variable glyph/word gaps, with no discrete printed tab-versus-space channel; the source-era ASCII carrier depends on literal space/tab bytes, which are not recoverable from finished JPEGs. - **Historical 2013 source material:** Cicada's original 761.MP3 ID3 text carrier contains the complete later-solved p57 PARABLE. An exact [original OpenPuff v4.00 native trial](../workplace/2013-parable-openpuff-crossround-source-gate.md) re-applied the genuinely successful 2014 Interconnectedness MP3 password `33011033` and MP3 Maximum 1/2 operation to the unaltered 2013 audio: [Windows run 43678061217](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/43678061217) revalidated the 745-byte 2014 positive then recorded a native `Task interrupted / corrupted stream` error with 976-byte target capacity and no extracted file. That one cross-round acquisition route is rejected; it provides no LP2 key, and no alternative author-selected OpenPuff settings are known. Its clause prime sums multiply to the associated 1,595,277,641 field value, while the title's three word sums total the original basename 761. These are verified source-level numeric correspondences, **not** a recovered cipher seed or a preceding-word→next-key handoff. The signed 2013 TCP protocol specifies BASE29/DH/NEXT but no NUMBER/PRIMES/COUNT commands or word-state cipher. The original DH exchange is reconstructed (request 3301; server base 2757/secret 331/public 1264; client share 2800/shared 1067), but six client exponents fit the base order and no LP2 key follows. The same unsigned participant transcript also returns seven session-random RAND bytes [151, 207, 64, 91, 172, 47, 179]; direct page-shift, period-7, BASE29-expanded and p56 φ-prime-start readings on the seven p33–39 segments fail (aggregate IoC 0.0342–0.0361 versus ciphertext baseline 0.03435), so the count match is not a usable key selector. [Exact vector and gate](../im-results/3301-1033-source-operation-audit.md#seven-rand-bytes-from-the-preserved-march-2013-tcp-session-page-key-gate) · [reproducer](../sims/lp2_p33_39_cuneiform_2013_rand7_key_gate.cjs). A second literal reading of the same transcript operands, `e=1033,n=3301`, as a two-rune modular-power FPE (29²=841 states; 840 pairs in the full p33–39 chapter; inverse exponent 1297) also fails in both block orders and both exponent directions; exhaustive inversion over all 841 states passes, but the outputs remain random (IoC 0.03439–0.03494). [Exact gate](../im-results/3301-1033-source-operation-audit.md#literal-2013-base29-operand-as-exponent-over-the-dh-modulus-two-rune-modular-power-fpe-gate) · [reproducer](../sims/lp2_cuneiform_1033_exponent_3301_modulus_fpe_sft.cjs). The NEXT transcript is a client upload with 01 OK only after its terminator, not a server-side key/page handoff. The complete original p33–39 cuneiform inventory is the same two base-60 values repeated in seven headers: [(p33,1033,3301),…,(p39,1033,3301)], not a new 14-value stream. The only other numerals are red passage labels 1–5 on p36–38; their 553-rune intervals have already failed source-motivated 5×5 masks, five-stream transposition, full matrices, the 1033^n mod3301 DH orbit, and the repaired exact p56 prime-totient/F-transparent inverse on as-printed, rune- and whole-word 1→5/5→1 streams under both signs (all ten variants fail; p57 positive plants pass; Node22 SFT 44527425170). No cuneiform→prime_echo link is established. The seven session-random bytes are a participant-transcript artifact, not a cuneiform-derived or organizer-selected key; the bounded consumers above fail. [2013 protocol source](../resources/external/2013-tcp-server-signed-source.md) · [NEXT directionality gate](../im-results/2013-next-chain-p33-39-gate.md) · [cuneiform source gate](../im-results/3301-1033-source-operation-audit.md). The legacy `0_wisdom` transcription is the 2014 LP1 p05/Stage04 artifact, not a 2013 signed clue. The signed 2016 words-map instruction does not supply an operator or key clock. A literal current-word reading, in which each printed word is shifted by the sum of its plaintext GP-prime values mod29, has now been tested in both directions: 1,049/1,050 original words have no fixed point, and only 159/54 of 2,896 match a source-native English dictionary whose coverage on three native controls is 94.4–98.1%. This rejects that exact source-keyed reading. A stronger **all-29-keys-per-word original exclusion (16 November)** removes *every* GP29 constant-Caesar-shift-per-printed-word schedule, even if its 2,896 shifts are selected independently or causally: the unchanged 12,956-rune book has **1,879/2,896 words with no normalized shape among 29,088 native-GP English words**, including **all 815 length-six-or-longer original words**. A complete 12,956-rune source-length-matched English plant under independently changing keys passes exact inversion and the same vocabulary gate. This is conditional on GP-native English and preserved printed word boundaries. A separate 29-start, per-word reset of the **authentic solved-p56 prime-totient cipher**, admitting both signs and all legal plaintext-F skip paths, fails at least **1,653/2,896** original words even with arbitrary favorable starts/signs, including all **815** original words of length six or more. Complete source-length-matched native-GP plants pass both gates. These exclusions do not constrain within-word evolving/independently larger prime starts, altered segmentation or word navigation. [Any-key SFT and scoped result](../im-results/lp2-wordwise-caesar-anyshift-original-gate.md) · [earlier fixed-key gate](../im-results/lp2-selfword-primesum-caesar-gate.md). **All-cut affine-word exclusion (16 November, Agent10):** even after moving every proposed plaintext word boundary arbitrarily over the complete original 12,956-rune tape, the independent 29,088-entry GP-English vocabulary has **zero affine-compatible contiguous source windows of any length 8–14** under all 812 possible GF29 affine keys at each position. Since the unchanged printed length multiset requires 343 words of these lengths, *every* length-multiset-preserving resegmentation under an independently keyed per-word affine alphabet fails this native-English necessity test. An independently rekeyed, resegmented 12,956-rune/2,896-word English plant passes exact inversion; arbitrary 29! secret per-word substitutions remain viable without additional source-constrained segmentation. A narrower exact gate on original physical **pp23–26** finds **0/1,008** contiguous 14-rune windows compatible with any of the 29,088-entry reference's length-14 GP-English equality patterns, although the intact group requires one length-14 word. Thus even independent unlimited 29! per-word keys and freely moved cuts **within that fixed group** fail this source-native-English register. This does not exclude word lengths transferred between groups, other vocabularies or changing rune order. [Existing report and consolidated SFT](../im-results/lp2-anycut-affine-wordlength-original-gate.md). Other plaintext registers, rune reordering and changed length profiles remain viable.

**All-secret polynomial wordshift (18 November, Agent31):** Original p39's red quadratic suggests testing a quadratic rune-position shift within words, though it supplies no such opcode. Grant each word independently arbitrary GP29 coefficients of degree 2 through 7. The `(degree+1)` finite difference cancels *every* secret polynomial, producing a key-free necessary test. Against 32,021 native-GP English word encodings from five pinned reference files, **no contiguous original cipher window of lengths 9–14** matches a degree-2 shifted native word, and the same all-cut zero gates begin at lengths **10/11/12/12/14** for degrees **3/4/5/6/7**. The manuscript requires respectively **187/109/57/25/25/3** printed words of these lengths or longer, so none of these complete per-word polynomial families can explain the unchanged rune order and printed length multiset in this language register. Exact planted decryptions pass. **Affine-scale extension to degrees 2–5:** grant every word all 28 secret nonzero GP29 multipliers as well as all polynomial coefficients. The normalized `(d+1)`th difference then has zero candidate original windows at length n≥11 (degrees 2–3, 57/57 required printed words), or n≥12 (degrees 4–5, 25/25 printed words); positive source-native plants pass ([same SFT](../sims/lp2_polynomial_wordshift_allsecret_sft.cjs), [Windows PASS 44305457452](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/44305457452)). This is a scoped lexical rejection, not a recovered plaintext or an author-selected operation ([original-source gate](../im-results/lp2_word_polynomial_allsecret_signature_original_gate.md) · [SFT](../sims/lp2_polynomial_wordshift_allsecret_sft.cjs)).
 [Source-exact gate](../im-results/lp2-anycut-affine-wordlength-original-gate.md) · [single-file reproducer](../sims/lp2_anycut_affine_wordlength_original_sft.cjs). A distinct lossless per-printed-word GP29 integer→bytes codec using the minimum fixed big-endian width b(n)=min{b:256^b≥29^n} was tested on original chapters p0–2 and p3–7: neither 525-byte nor 821-byte stream has a byte-0 format signature, valid JSON or qualifying UTF-8 text; both have 0/100,000 SplitMix64 null recognitions (95% one-sided upper bound 2.996×10⁻⁵). This rejects only that exact word-field transport; the 2013 source authenticates BASE29 integer conversion, not this byte serialization. [Exact gate](../im-results/lp2-word-base29-fixed-field-bytes-gate.md). The 73–1223 gap in the first-onion telnet prime output is documented in a **9 May 2016 participant wiki revision**, which names the original Pastebin capture `xGY4pbHV` and its unusual spacing; a separate 2018 historical GitHub transcription also records the jump. This pre-2022 witness still cannot authenticate the original 2013 server behavior or an author-selected key: the only recovered `server.py` is a December 2022 community reconstruction that hardcodes the gap, and the distinct CicadaOS ISO `prime_echo` script contains all 464 primes through 3301. That ISO script pauses for two seconds at 1033 and 3301, exactly the 2013 BASE29 vectors 16I and 3QO; these are deliberate 2013 anchors, but the telnet gap remains unverified and 1033 has no established LP2 consumer. Treat the gap as an observed output feature, not key material, until contemporaneous server evidence is found. [Source audit](../im-results/2013-cicadaos-telnet-prime-gap-source-audit.md). [Source-register benchmark](../im-results/source-register-benchmark.md), [historical protocol](../resources/external/2013-tcp-server-signed-source.md), and [preceding-word handoff audit](../im-results/lp2-word-prime-feedback-historical-precedent-gate.md). The original **76,639 May–August 2014 dated IRC lines** contain no exact source reference to `DATA/560.13` or `560.17`; the January 2016 `560.17`/first-17-pages and `560.13`/761.mp3 remarks are **participant suggestions**, without a signed LP2 receiver ([original-log source gate](../im-results/lp2-2013-56013-dense-direct-offset-gate.md)). The genuine **1,183,811-byte 2013 `DATA/560.17`** has been byte-identically recovered in separate **May 2021** and **December 2022** public archives, SHA256 `d5676b1e01fd371cb13415b59b2eca4336e96f7bb05e789198b8d6baab580c19`. The 2021 `.rev` is an **exact whole-file byte reversal**, not a second key; the original **2013 Dataset17 XOR** recovers two independently recorded historical onion addresses with source offsets 33,461 and 617. These authenticate its **historical 2013 operation**, while the original source provides **no 2014 LP2 byte-to-rune/offset selection** ([consolidated 2013-source and LP2-negative report](../im-results/lp2-2013-cicadaos-authored-offset-skip-and-xor.md), [cross-archive reproduction](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/actions/runs/43851837497), [Issue #1247](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1247)).

**Stage10 image-number cross-round gates (#1638/#1648, Agents1/6, 18 November).** The preserved 152-byte Stage10 success response returns exactly `107.jpg`, `167.jpg`, `229.jpg`. Their one-based prime ranks are **28,39,50**, an exact step-11 progression, while reduction by the independently p27-selected DH modulus23 gives **15,6,22**. Both cross-round readings now fail independent selection. The historical filename family has no second comparable fixed three-number response, so there is no matched source-preserving replication. The prime ranks require three different LP2 data types (local rune offset, table-token contents, physical page). For mod23, the signed 2013 transcript exposes server public8 before client public19, whose base5 private logs are **6 then15**; Stage10 instead orders **15 then6**, then appends derived group order22. The three returned files are independently byte-identical to consecutive LP1 pages **14,15,16 in that exact order**, giving their order a direct source explanation. An explicitly unmatched control over LP2 labels0–57 has zero matching consecutive triples; among all 30,856 increasing 3-subsets exactly `(15,29,45)` shares the signature, which is descriptive only. Preserve the arithmetic, while retiring both as cross-round selectors absent a first-party operation and role/address rule ([consolidated gate](../im-results/2014-stage10-prime-rank-crossround-gate.md) · [mod23 SFT](../sims/2014_stage10_mod23_dh_role_order_sft.cjs) · [#1648](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1648)).
**Stage10 LP1 p14–16 literal-content gate (#1662, Agent6, 18 November).** The exact Stage10 payload has a coherent local reading in solved source order: p14 teaches that “THE I IS THE VOICE OF THE CIRCUMFERENCE” and locates that voice inside the head; p15 explicitly says `DISCOVER TRUTH INSIDE YOURSELF`; p16 says `KNOW THIS` and prints the 5×5 square whose rows, columns and principal diagonals sum3301. These are authentic historical circumference/inside cues, and p16 directly closes the Stage10 magic-square task. They do not select p27, the p49–51 table, p55, or the signed 2016 message, and they specify no reversible LP2 operation. The strongest direct cross-release object relation is instead the already-established mirrored reuse of the shared pp14–16 reclining footer on LP2 p22; existing source-derived p22 cipher transfers are negative. Preserve the triplet as the authenticated Stage10 output and as thematic context, while closing a direct pp14–16→LP2-operation handoff until a new first-party cue selects both the object and operation ([gate](../im-results/2014-stage10-lp1-p14-16-literal-lp2-bridge-gate.md) · [exact Stage10 identity](../im-results/2014-stage10-three-images-lp1-source-handoff.md) · [#1662](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1662)).

**Stage10 LP1-triplet visual-layout gate (#1663, Agent4, 18 November).** Original-image comparison closes the remaining pp14–16→pp49–51 template hypothesis. The Stage10 pages have heterogeneous ordered layouts: p14 has **11 rune-text lines**; p15 has **5 rune-text lines plus paired insects**; p16 has **4 rune-text lines, an insect, a heading and a 5×5 square**. LP2 pp49–51 instead contain one continuous **32×8** table split **10/13/9** rows, with a different mirrored-tree frame. No source-natural line count, column count, seam, margin, crop, scale or reflection maps the Stage10 triplet onto that table. Repeated three-page framing is already common within LP1 (pp6–9 and pp10–13 supply controls), while the exact cross-release raster reuse of the Stage10 reclining footer occurs on **LP2 p22**, not the table pages. Combined with the older failed 5×5/7×7/5×5 square→pp49–51 numerical gate, retire Stage10 pp14–16 as a p50 layout/key source absent a new first-party instruction ([visual gate](../im-results/2014-stage10-lp1-triplet-lp2-table-layout-gate.md) · [older fixed-layout gate](../im-results/2014-stage10-three-square-pp49-51-original-fixed-layout-gate.md)).

- **Recovered embedded 2014 square in LP1 p05:** The original *SOME WISDOM* JPEG is `A || M || reverse(A)`, where `M` is a two-newline/358-hex-character central block. Its 179 binary bytes reversibly decode by **nibble swapping each byte and reversing each decimal token** into the exact **third independently preserved historical 2014 five-by-five numerical square**. All 25 cells match the already-known [2014 magic-square source](../resources/cicada2014-openpuff-magic-squares.txt), each row/column/both diagonals **independently sum to the original LP1 Wisdom constant 1033**, and the entire 360-byte original center can be re-encoded. The **same 25 numerical cells are already printed on LP1 p05 as a mixed square**: 12 cells are written as numbers and 13 as GP rune words whose rune-prime sums give the remaining numbers. The original 2014 Stage10 `/cgi-bin/squares` form accepted this already-known third square as the order-five/1033 submission (`51033`), alongside two other squares and a participant onion address; the preserved success response led to **LP1 pages 14–16**. A separate original 10 February 2014 IRC witness records a live Onion6 /cgi-bin/ directory listing with an entry named squares* at size 965; the original CGI executable, POST bytes and rejected-input behavior remain unavailable, so the listing does not reveal a square decoder. All three recovered magic squares had a typed Stage10 submission role; the two 5×5 squares also have LP1 p05/p16 source roles, while the 7×7 has no separate LP1 origin. No contemporaneous LP2 square consumer is authenticated. The Stage 10 5×5 3301 square has exact GF(29) order 56 (determinant 10); the 5×5 1033 square has order 28 (determinant 3); and the 7×7 1033 square has order **280 = 5×56** (determinant 2). The two 5×5 matrices do not commute and the 1033 matrix is not a power of the 3301 matrix. We tested 32 page-indexed, block-indexed, and matrix-orbit consumers of the 5×5 squares, plus 20 fixed/page/block/orbit consumers of the 7×7 square. All 52 variants leave 12,956 runes ciphertext-like: pooled IoC 0.03445–0.03454 versus 0.06271 on clear p57. The order relationships are genuine structural facts, but these direct consumers are negative and do not establish authorial intent. [Stage 10 matrix-order/key-schedule report](../im-results/lp1-p16-order56-pagepower-gate.md) · [reproducer](../sims/lp1_p16_order56_pagepower_sft.cjs). [Original listing/source boundary](../im-results/2014-stage10-squares-cgi-source-boundary.md). Thus the hidden binary recovers an authenticated *redundant representation of a solved-stage operand*, **without deciphering any new LP2 rune or identifying a table-to-key instruction**. [Original mixed-square conversion and historical navigation-rule gate](../im-results/lp1-p05-word-number-direction-source-gate.md) · [Stage10 form and response](../im-results/2014-stage10-squares-cgi-source-boundary.md). One frozen direct transfer, `φ(hiddenGrid[row-major mod25]) mod29` as a 75-rune GP29 shift key with p55's F at local25 selected as the sole interrupt among **three** original ciphertext Fs (local **15, 25 and 41**), yields nonprose on all original 76 p55 runes (IoC **0.0333333333**), despite exact reencryption. The one-of-three F bypass is an **unverified mask choice**, so this is a scoped rejection of that conditional key consumer, not of other skip masks or table uses. The authentic 73-line Stage10 client HTML has already been recovered: a plain URL-encoded POST form targeting `/cgi-bin/squares`, with named fields `53301`, `71033`, `51033` and `onion`; it contains no client JavaScript or hidden transform ([closed #1244](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1244); [original source boundary](../im-results/2014-stage10-squares-cgi-source-boundary.md)). The original 965-byte server CGI, exact accepted POST body and rejected-input behavior remain unrecovered ([closed #1228](https://github.com/FlareZ123/liber-primus/issues/1228)); no source-authenticated LP2 decoder follows.

**Exact A/I/O exception bound refining the preexisting [whole-word collision exclusion](../im-results/lp2-word-collision-injective-source-gate.md) (Agent11, 14 November 2026).** For every deterministic, length-preserving word cipher that starts each printed word in the same key/state per book, visual chapter or physical page, three possible ordinary-English standalone GP words A/I/O imply at most three distinct singleton ciphertext outputs per scope. Original LP2 has 83 one-rune words using 28 glyphs; even granting independent keys by physical page, pages 17, 19 and 28 force at least four non-A/I/O standalone words. The forced minima are 68 bookwide and 45 across nine visual chapters. Word-varying or context-dependent state, changed segmentation and different registers survive. No plaintext or key recovered. [Exact generalization](../im-results/lp2-wordreset-fivephase-shared-allsecret-original-gate.md#general-deterministic-whole-word-reset-singleton-capacity-theorem). The original 83 singleton words now give a stronger **all-key recovery obstruction to every direct-additive homogeneous or affine fourth-order GP29 running-key recurrence** under the A/I/O English singleton assumption. The original whole-book homogeneous inverse rejects **707,281/707,281** coefficient tuples by the thirteenth singleton, forcing NG. Independently, the original p15–22 visual chapter alone contains **18** singleton words and rejects **all 707,281 affine coefficient tuples** by its fourteenth even when each chapter gets a completely fresh four-rune seed and free constant intercept; the last candidate forces EO at original offset4,751. This is a preserved-boundary, constant-within-chapter, all-runes-consuming model exclusion. The same exact affine-degree-four reduction covers **every 4×4 affine GF29 state matrix** with any fixed linear readout and arbitrary secret state (Cayley–Hamilton). Even granting a **fully unknown nonlinear 29-entry output lookup** of either source-linked p15 matrix-characteristic or signed-2013 H·p39 G recurrence, every chapter-local seed that survives the singleton test gives **original conditional IoC at most 0.036749** over the correct 1,894-rune p15:9–p22 red-rubric body, while three source-native GP-English controls stay **at least 0.057360** under those same candidate seed partitions. This is an empirical native-prose exclusion for these two specified nonlinear clocks, while non-GP encoding, punctuation remapping, changing clocks or other ciphers remain open. [Complete all-secret singleton inverse and conditional nonlinear chapter gate](../im-results/lp2-all-order4-singleton-aio-exact-inverse.md).

**Additional source-native five-step gate (14 November, Agent 7):** Reusing an arbitrary nonlinear GP29 substitution alphabet at each of five *word-relative* phases, restarting phase at every authentic printed word, cannot restore tested native-English GP frequencies even when the five alphabets are independently secret per book, nine visual chapters or 55 physical pages. The exact all-key phase collision rates are **0.03439991 / 0.03440024 / 0.03460189**, while 450 authentic word-length-matched literary controls at each scope have minima **0.06933–0.07142**, with 500 solved-Cicada word-pool controls also above the original. **True SPACE-token capacity with arbitrary moved word boundaries (Agent26, 15 November 2026).** The printed LP2 separators may be completely unrelated to the plaintext's word boundaries. For the competing source-token model of 29 GP rune symbols plus true SPACE, each fixed codeword has at most two emitted GP29 digits, optionally changed by secret codebook at book, nine original chapters, physical pages, or a public rune-phase clock. Regardless of all secret alphabet mappings, each SPACE begins with one phase-specific output digit. Summing the actual **maximum phase-specific source-symbol counts** therefore bounds the number of genuine spaces. Across all **12,956 original runes**, the authentic solved p57 spacing density **19/114** requires a codebook clock period at least **152** globally, **18** with the nine visual-chapter resets, or **3** with independently keyed physical pages for cumulative GP29 differences (raw-digit periods **164/20/4**). Including the independent native solved p56 layout (**85 runes, 25 authentic words**) gives pooled p56+p57 **43/223** SPACE-token density; respective cumulative clock thresholds rise to **237/27/5**. These are **conditional necessary length-and-register bounds**, not a universal prohibition or decipherment. Multi-rune mixing, secret position-dependent states and a plaintext register with different spacing remain open. A source-frozen prefix decoder recovers **114/114** authentic p57 GP-plus-SPACE tokens but fails unchanged original p54–55, p3–7 and p0–2. **Historical ABAAB floral selector and exact two-alphabet limit (14 November, Agent30):** A surviving **16 February 2016** original solver transcript names the flower-berry five-position color word `ABAAB` and explicitly gives its Beatty addresses `floor((5N−1)/3)`; the actual five-dot p55 cluster reproduces that clock. The source does **not** prescribe a decryption. A new exact original-cipher test of the economical reversible reading, **two independent secret GP29 bijections per physical page**, chosen by A/B positions with **any of five phase rotations on each page**, proves that the nine original floral pages **p40–48 (2,408 runes)** can decrypt to at most **174** instances of any one GP plaintext symbol. A distinct **aperiodic Fibonacci-word selector** motivated by the independently original p15 Fibonacci spiral and the floral `ABAAB` prefix permits every possible Fibonacci-word start independently on each page and still caps original E at **187/2,408**, below the same independently pinned Shakespeare minimum **196**. The **13 original p05 GP rune-word cells**, read in physical row-major order without its twelve numeric cells, concatenate to **exactly 76 GP runes**, equal to the complete p55 ciphertext length on the repeated-moth page. This gives a source-derived complete-length candidate 76-rune key tape without fitted phase or skipped F runes. Six original-input GP29 additive inverses using its canonical rune indices, prime values, or totients (both signs) all remain nonlinguistic (IoC **0.03018–0.04105**), despite exact forward reconstruction and source-native solved-p57 plants. The same moth also occurs on p53; the original source does not instruct using this 76-rune tape as a key. Moreover, the exact **p56-authenticated plaintext-F bypass rule** rules out the 76-rune tape as a direct φ(prime) running key: all **eight** possible bypass masks for p55's three ciphertext Fs fail in each of the printed/reversed source orders. Under the printed order, non-F ciphertext TH at offset13 must decrypt to forbidden plaintext F before any optional skip; under the fully reversed order, non-F U at offset20 must likewise become F regardless of the earlier F15 choice. This is an exact cipher-rule contradiction, not just a language score. [Original-source SFT](../sims/lp1_p05_wordcells76_p55_exact_sourcekey_sft.cjs) · [consolidated p05 original-artifact analysis](../im-results/lp1-page05-reversed-prefix-anomaly.md#exact-source-born-76-rune-word-tape-and-moth-linked-p55-14-november-agent-2). This is distinct from the 13-column numeric transposition and XOR5 gates; it retires only these eight direct prime-totient tape orders. Original p55-to-logo registration gives **561/580 SIFT inliers** and fuzzy ink F1 **0.99405**; original p53→p55 copies the same emblem with a **(+66,+67)-pixel** displacement and no detectable p55-only high-contrast drawn message. This authentic image reuse does **not** select a 76-rune permutation, caption key, plaintext crib, or decoding operation. The original-moth geometry/permutation investigation **#1238** and external source-plate investigation **#1249** are closed; the source-derived 76-rune tape tests above remain bounded negatives. ## 2. Cryptanalytic constraints that affect the next attack

**Original-image geometry is a closed ordinary-typesetting channel (Agent10, 21 November 2026).** The OCR-free source-pixel atlas spans all physical pages p0–57 and solved controls. Clean line geometry gives left starts x=600–603, ordinary 188/189-px baseline pitch and common right edge x=1792; the seven 224-px leads are four within-word, two between-word and one displaced-period cases, and the same spacing occurs in LP1. The remaining-page micro-layout census finds 319/340 standard-pitch intervals, 13 visible 224-px paragraph leads, five 243.5/244-px section gaps and three artwork breaks; all 1,623 accepted small-dot marks are 9 or 10 px. The 25-page same-rune vertical test finds only 10/2,179 whole-glyph ±1-px events in original images versus 1,457/2,179 in a planted positive. No source-selected coordinate grammar, key or receiver survives; the stable measurements are explained by ordinary composition and font geometry.
